Could you tell me what brings you in today?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 48 / stocker_or_order_filler / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Hallucinations\nmanifestation: \"What scared me enough to come in is that I’ve been hearing my name called from the hallway at home and once at the warehouse when nobody was there, and it sounds as real as my husband standing right next to me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um… yeah. I came in because something has been really scaring me, and I kept trying to tell myself it was just the house settling or maybe I was tired, but it’s gotten hard to ignore.

I’ve been hearing my name called from the hallway at home when nobody’s there. Like, not in my head exactly—it sounds real, like someone is actually standing out there saying my name. A couple times I thought it was my husband, because it sounded that close, like he was right next to me or just around the corner, but when I checked, nobody was there.

And then it happened once at the warehouse too, where I work, and that really shook me up because I was sure someone had called me, but there wasn’t anyone there. I mean, it sounded as real as if my husband was standing right beside me. That’s what scared me enough to come in.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think I’ve seen anything, no… not like seeing a person standing there or something crawling on me. It’s really been the hearing part that’s bothering me.

It’s my name. I’ll be at home and I’ll hear it from the hallway, clear as anything, like someone is right there just out of sight. And I’ll stop what I’m doing because it doesn’t sound like a thought—it sounds like an actual voice in the house. A couple times I honestly thought it was my husband calling me, because it was that real, like he was standing right next to me or just around the corner. But then I go look, and there’s nobody there.

And it happened once at the warehouse too, which really scared me because at work there’s always noises and people moving around, so at first I thought, okay, someone must’ve called me. But when I checked, no one had. That’s the part I can’t shake—how real it sounds when there’s nobody there.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think I thought someone was trying to hurt me, no… not like that. But I guess there was a time I got really, really sure about this idea I had, and looking back I can see other people didn’t quite understand why I was so convinced.

I do crafts, you know, little things I make, and I got it in my head that I could turn it into a real business right away. Not just selling a few things here and there, but like… this was going to be our retirement plan, and I had figured it out. I remember feeling like everyone would see it once I explained it properly, like I had finally found the perfect way to set our family up. It felt obvious to me at the time, like, “Why hasn’t anyone else thought of this?” And I was pretty sure I could make it work fast, not someday way down the line.

I don’t know if that counts as unusual. It didn’t feel strange then. It felt smart, like I was finally seeing the whole picture. But I know my family didn’t react like I expected. I think they thought I was getting ahead of myself.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think the closest thing would be the crafts idea I was talking about. At the time it didn’t feel like a “belief” the way you’re saying it now—it felt like I had finally figured something out that everyone else just wasn’t catching up to yet.

I make crafts, and I got really convinced that I could turn it into a real business right away. Not slowly, not like a little side thing, but like something that could actually become our retirement plan. I remember thinking, very clearly, that this was the perfect plan for our family, like I had worked out all the pieces in my head and once I explained it properly, everyone would see it too.

And when they didn’t… I didn’t take that as a sign that maybe I was overestimating it. I just felt like they weren’t understanding what I was seeing. I was so sure I had figured it out, like it was obvious and they were being too cautious or negative. Looking back, I can see I was probably way more certain than made sense, but at the time I really believed it.

  "note": "The patient reports auditory hallucinations that sound real and occur in the absence of external stimuli. There is no evidence of visual hallucinations or other sensory delusions. The patient does describe a past episode of a fixed, false belief (delusion) regarding a craft business being a retirement plan, which others did not share. This may represent a brief delusional episode. The hallucinations are non-command and isolated, without clear evidence of a primary psychotic disorder like schizophrenia (F20.x) at this time. This supports a possible diagnosis of an acute or transient psychotic disorder (F23.2, F23.3, F23.8, F23.9) or schizoaffective disorder (F25.0–F25.9), given the possible affective component with the prior elevated mood/overvalued idea. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, that definitely happened. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve called it “unusually energetic” at the time, because I thought I was just finally getting things done, you know? But looking back, it was like I couldn’t sit still.

I’d come home from the warehouse after being on my feet all day, and instead of being tired like I normally would be, I’d start tearing through closets or reorganizing things that didn’t really need to be reorganized right then. I’d be pulling boxes out, making piles, deciding we needed a whole new system for things. And then I’d sit down and start rewriting schedules, like household schedules or plans for what needed to get done, and I’d keep changing them because another idea would come to me.

I was also looking up genealogy records, just going and going with it, like I had to keep following every little lead right then or I’d lose it. And I was making lists constantly—lists for the house, lists for projects, lists for things to look up, things to buy, things to fix. It felt like my brain was moving faster than my body, honestly. I’d finish one thing and immediately think of three more things I needed to do.

Other people probably just saw me as being busy or worked up, but inside it felt more intense than that. Like there wasn’t an off switch.

I think they thought I was getting ahead of myself.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you strongly believed something that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to explain otherwise, you still felt certain it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think the closest thing would be the crafts idea I was talking about. I just felt like they weren’t understanding what I was seeing. I was so sure I had figured it out, like it was obvious and they were being too cautious or negative. Looking back, I can see I was probably way more certain than made sense, but at the time I really believed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing, even if others around you didn’t seem to notice or understand what you were experiencing?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that definitely happened. I’d finish one thing and immediately think of three more things I needed to do.\n\nOther people probably just saw me as being busy or worked up, but inside it felt more intense than that. Not really like having a special connection to someone famous, no. I didn’t think a celebrity was sending me messages or anything like that. It was more… with my crafts, I got this really strong belief that I had figured out something big.

I make things, and I started thinking I could turn it into a real business almost right away—not just extra money, but something serious, like it could be our retirement plan. And I remember feeling like I had this perfect plan for the family, like all the pieces made sense in my head, and if I could just explain it the right way, everyone else would see it too.

It’s hard to describe because at the time it didn’t feel strange. It felt like confidence, like I was finally seeing what needed to happen. But looking back, I can see I was maybe putting way too much certainty on it. I really believed I had figured out the answer for us, and I was frustrated when other people didn’t understand why it seemed so obvious to me.

No… I don’t think so. Not like the TV was talking to me or a random comment had some hidden meaning for me personally. I mean, sometimes if you’re already worked up about something, you notice things more—like if I’m thinking about crafts, then I’ll see craft ideas everywhere, or if someone mentions money or retirement I might think, “See, that’s exactly what I’ve been saying.” But I don’t think I felt like it was directed at me in some special way.

It was more that I was focused on my own ideas, if that makes sense. Like I was interpreting things through whatever I was already thinking about, but I wasn’t sitting there believing the news or a show or strangers were sending me messages. I don’t remember anything like that.

I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve called it “unusually energetic” at the time, because I thought I was just finally getting things done, you know? I don’t remember anything like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a period of time where you believed something strongly that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to tell you otherwise, you couldn’t let go of that belief—like thinking something was going to happen, that someone had a particular intention, or that you had a special role to play?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think that’s probably the crafts thing again, because at the time I really, really believed I had figured something out. It wasn’t just, “Oh, maybe I could sell a few things,” it was more like I had this whole picture in my head where it was going to become a real business right away, like a serious thing, and it could be our retirement plan.

And when I say it out loud now, I know that sounds like a big jump. But back then it felt so clear to me. I had ideas for what I would make, how I’d sell it, how the money would build up, how it would help the family. I kept thinking, if everyone would just listen long enough, they’d understand that I had the perfect plan. Like I had solved something for us.

If someone questioned it or acted unsure, I didn’t really take that in. I’d just think they weren’t seeing the whole picture yet, or I hadn’t explained it the right way. I couldn’t let it go because it felt obvious to me, like I was the only one looking at it correctly. Looking back, I can see I was way too certain about it, but at the time I believed it completely.

That’s the part that feels so real and makes me nervous.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts or beliefs that others might find unusual or hard to understand, like thinking someone is trying to harm you or that you have a special ability others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I thought someone was trying to hurt me, no… not like that. It sounds like someone is really there—like my husband is standing right next to me or just around the corner—but then there’s nobody. And it happened once at the warehouse too, which really shook me because I thought someone had called me at work, but nobody had. That’s the part that feels so real and makes me nervous.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts or beliefs that others might find unusual or hard to understand, like thinking someone is trying to harm you or that you have a special ability others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I thought someone was trying to hurt me, no… not like that. But I guess there was a time I got really, really sure about this idea I had, and looking back I can see other people didn’t quite understand why I was so convinced.\n\nI do crafts, you know, little things I make, and I got it in my head that I could turn it into a real business right away. Not just selling a few things here and there, but like… this was going to be our retirement plan, and I had figured it out. I remember feeling like everyone would see it once I explained it properly, like I had finally found the perfect way to set our family up. It felt obvious to me at the time, like, “Why hasn’t anyone else thought of this?” And I was pretty sure I could make it work fast, not someday way down the line.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as unusual. It didn’t feel strange then. It felt smart, like I was finally seeing the whole picture. But I know my family didn’t react like I expected. I think they thought I was getting ahead of myself.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you strongly believed something that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to explain otherwise, you still felt certain it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think the closest thing would be the crafts idea I was talking about. At the time it didn’t feel like a “belief” the way you’re saying it now—it felt like I had finally figured something out that everyone else just wasn’t catching up to yet.\n\nI make crafts, and I got really convinced that I could turn it into a real business right away. Not slowly, not like a little side thing, but like something that could actually become our retirement plan. I remember thinking, very clearly, that this was the perfect plan for our family, like I had worked out all the pieces in my head and once I explained it properly, everyone would see it too.\n\nAnd when they didn’t… I didn’t take that as a sign that maybe I was overestimating it. I just felt like they weren’t understanding what I was seeing. I was so sure I had figured it out, like it was obvious and they were being too cautious or negative. Looking back, I can see I was probably way more certain than made sense, but at the time I really believed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing, even if others around you didn’t seem to notice or understand what you were experiencing?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that definitely happened. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve called it “unusually energetic” at the time, because I thought I was just finally getting things done, you know? But looking back, it was like I couldn’t sit still.\n\nI’d come home from the warehouse after being on my feet all day, and instead of being tired like I normally would be, I’d start tearing through closets or reorganizing things that didn’t really need to be reorganized right then. I’d be pulling boxes out, making piles, deciding we needed a whole new system for things. And then I’d sit down and start rewriting schedules, like household schedules or plans for what needed to get done, and I’d keep changing them because another idea would come to me.\n\nI was also looking up genealogy records, just going and going with it, like I had to keep following every little lead right then or I’d lose it. And I was making lists constantly—lists for the house, lists for projects, lists for things to look up, things to buy, things to fix. It felt like my brain was moving faster than my body, honestly. I’d finish one thing and immediately think of three more things I needed to do.\n\nOther people probably just saw me as being busy or worked up, but inside it felt more intense than that. Like there wasn’t an off switch.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others might not share—like thinking you had a special connection to someone famous, or that events in the world were directly related to you in a way that others didn’t understand?\n\nPatient: Not really like having a special connection to someone famous, no. I didn’t think a celebrity was sending me messages or anything like that. It was more… with my crafts, I got this really strong belief that I had figured out something big.\n\nI make things, and I started thinking I could turn it into a real business almost right away—not just extra money, but something serious, like it could be our retirement plan. And I remember feeling like I had this perfect plan for the family, like all the pieces made sense in my head, and if I could just explain it the right way, everyone else would see it too.\n\nIt’s hard to describe because at the time it didn’t feel strange. I really believed I had figured out the answer for us, and I was frustrated when other people didn’t understand why it seemed so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things in the environment—like things on TV, things people say around you, or even random events—were directed at you in some way, like they had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes if you’re already worked up about something, you notice things more—like if I’m thinking about crafts, then I’ll see craft ideas everywhere, or if someone mentions money or retirement I might think, “See, that’s exactly what I’ve been saying.” But I don’t think I felt like it was directed at me in some special way.\n\nIt was more that I was focused on my own ideas, if that makes sense. Like I was interpreting things through whatever I was already thinking about, but I wasn’t sitting there believing the news or a show or strangers were sending me messages. I don’t remember anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you believed something strongly that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to tell you otherwise, you couldn’t let go of that belief—like thinking something was going to happen, that someone had a particular intention, or that you had a special role to play?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably the crafts thing again, because at the time I really, really believed I had figured something out. Not so much smells or tastes, no. I can’t think of anything like tasting something strange or smelling smoke or perfume when there wasn’t anything there.

But… the thing that really scared me, honestly, and part of why I came in, is I’ve been hearing my name called. Like from the hallway at home. I’ll be in another room and I’ll hear it clear as anything, like somebody is standing there calling me. And I’ll go look, or I’ll ask my husband if he said something, and nobody did.

It happened once at the warehouse too, and that really shook me because I’m used to noise there, you know—forklifts, people moving around, all that—but this was different. It sounded like my name, clear and close, and when I checked there wasn’t anyone there who could’ve said it. It didn’t sound like a thought in my head. It sounded real, like my husband standing right next to me saying my name. That’s what scared me.

But I guess there was a time I got really, really sure about this idea I had, and looking back I can see other people didn’t quite understand why I was so convinced.\n\nI do crafts, you know, little things I make, and I got it in my head that I could turn it into a real business right away. Not just selling a few things here and there, but like… this was going to be our retirement plan, and I had figured it out. I remember feeling like everyone would see it once I explained it properly, like I had finally found the perfect way to set our family up. It felt obvious to me at the time, like, “Why hasn’t anyone else thought of this?” And I was pretty sure I could make it work fast, not someday way down the line.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as unusual. It didn’t feel strange then. It felt smart, like I was finally seeing the whole picture. But I know my family didn’t react like I expected. I think they thought I was getting ahead of myself.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you strongly believed something that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to explain otherwise, you still felt certain it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think the closest thing would be the crafts idea I was talking about. At the time it didn’t feel like a “belief” the way you’re saying it now—it felt like I had finally figured something out that everyone else just wasn’t catching up to yet.\n\nI make crafts, and I got really convinced that I could turn it into a real business right away. Not slowly, not like a little side thing, but like something that could actually become our retirement plan. I remember thinking, very clearly, that this was the perfect plan for our family, like I had worked out all the pieces in my head and once I explained it properly, everyone would see it too.\n\nAnd when they didn’t… I didn’t take that as a sign that maybe I was overestimating it. I just felt like they weren’t understanding what I was seeing. I was so sure I had figured it out, like it was obvious and they were being too cautious or negative. Looking back, I can see I was probably way more certain than made sense, but at the time I really believed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing, even if others around you didn’t seem to notice or understand what you were experiencing?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that definitely happened. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve called it “unusually energetic” at the time, because I thought I was just finally getting things done, you know? But looking back, it was like I couldn’t sit still.\n\nI’d come home from the warehouse after being on my feet all day, and instead of being tired like I normally would be, I’d start tearing through closets or reorganizing things that didn’t really need to be reorganized right then. I’d be pulling boxes out, making piles, deciding we needed a whole new system for things. And then I’d sit down and start rewriting schedules, like household schedules or plans for what needed to get done, and I’d keep changing them because another idea would come to me.\n\nI was also looking up genealogy records, just going and going with it, like I had to keep following every little lead right then or I’d lose it. And I was making lists constantly—lists for the house, lists for projects, lists for things to look up, things to buy, things to fix. It felt like my brain was moving faster than my body, honestly. I’d finish one thing and immediately think of three more things I needed to do.\n\nOther people probably just saw me as being busy or worked up, but inside it felt more intense than that. Like there wasn’t an off switch.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others might not share—like thinking you had a special connection to someone famous, or that events in the world were directly related to you in a way that others didn’t understand?\n\nPatient: Not really like having a special connection to someone famous, no. I didn’t think a celebrity was sending me messages or anything like that. It was more… with my crafts, I got this really strong belief that I had figured out something big.\n\nI make things, and I started thinking I could turn it into a real business almost right away—not just extra money, but something serious, like it could be our retirement plan. And I remember feeling like I had this perfect plan for the family, like all the pieces made sense in my head, and if I could just explain it the right way, everyone else would see it too.\n\nIt’s hard to describe because at the time it didn’t feel strange. It felt like confidence, like I was finally seeing what needed to happen. But looking back, I can see I was maybe putting way too much certainty on it. I really believed I had figured out the answer for us, and I was frustrated when other people didn’t understand why it seemed so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things in the environment—like things on TV, things people say around you, or even random events—were directed at you in some way, like they had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like the TV was talking to me or a random comment had some hidden meaning for me personally. I mean, sometimes if you’re already worked up about something, you notice things more—like if I’m thinking about crafts, then I’ll see craft ideas everywhere, or if someone mentions money or retirement I might think, “See, that’s exactly what I’ve been saying.” But I don’t think I felt like it was directed at me in some special way.\n\nIt was more that I was focused on my own ideas, if that makes sense. Like I was interpreting things through whatever I was already thinking about, but I wasn’t sitting there believing the news or a show or strangers were sending me messages. I don’t remember anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you believed something strongly that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to tell you otherwise, you couldn’t let go of that belief—like thinking something was going to happen, that someone had a particular intention, or that you had a special role to play?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably the crafts thing again, because at the time I really, really believed I had figured something out. It wasn’t just, “Oh, maybe I could sell a few things,” it was more like I had this whole picture in my head where it was going to become a real business right away, like a serious thing, and it could be our retirement plan.\n\nAnd when I say it out loud now, I know that sounds like a big jump. But back then it felt so clear to me. I had ideas for what I would make, how I’d sell it, how the money would build up, how it would help the family. I kept thinking, if everyone would just listen long enough, they’d understand that I had the perfect plan. Like I had solved something for us.\n\nIf someone questioned it or acted unsure, I didn’t really take that in. I’d just think they weren’t seeing the whole picture yet, or I hadn’t explained it the right way. I couldn’t let it go because it felt obvious to me, like I was the only one looking at it correctly. Looking back, I can see I was way too certain about it, but at the time I believed it completely.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other types of unusual perceptions, like smelling something that wasn’t there, tasting something odd without a cause, or feeling physical sensations that had no clear explanation?\n\nPatient: Not so much smells or tastes, no. I can’t think of anything like tasting something strange or smelling smoke or perfume when there wasn’t anything there.\n\nBut… the thing that really scared me, honestly, and part of why I came in, is I’ve been hearing my name called. Like from the hallway at home. I’ll be in another room and I’ll hear it clear as anything, like somebody is standing there calling me. And I’ll go look, or I’ll ask my husband if he said something, and nobody did.\n\nIt happened once at the warehouse too, and that really shook me because I’m used to noise there, you know—forklifts, people moving around, all that—but this was different. It sounded like my name, clear and close, and when I checked there wasn’t anyone there who could’ve said it. It didn’t sound like a thought in my head. It sounded real, like my husband standing right next to me saying my name. That’s what scared me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I guess I did feel that way, mostly around the crafts idea. At the time it didn’t feel like I was being unrealistic or bragging or anything. It felt like I had finally figured out something important, like I saw a way forward that everyone else just wasn’t seeing yet.

I really believed I could turn my crafts into a real business right away, not just a little side hobby. In my head it was going to become this serious thing that could help us retire, help the family, kind of prove that I had found the answer. I remember thinking that once people saw what I was making and how I planned it out, they’d understand I had this perfect plan.

And if my family didn’t get it right away, I’d feel frustrated, like, “Why can’t they see this?” I wasn’t thinking, “Maybe I’m getting ahead of myself.” I was thinking they just didn’t recognize yet how good the idea was, or how capable I was of making it happen. Looking back, it was a lot more confidence than I usually have, and it got big in my head really fast.

Not slowly, not like a little side thing, but like something that could actually become our retirement plan. I just felt like they weren’t understanding what I was seeing. I was so sure I had figured it out, like it was obvious and they were being too cautious or negative. Looking back, I can see I was probably way more certain than made sense, but at the time I really believed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing, even if others around you didn’t seem to notice or understand what you were experiencing?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that definitely happened. I’d be pulling boxes out, making piles, deciding we needed a whole new system for things. And then I’d sit down and start rewriting schedules, like household schedules or plans for what needed to get done, and I’d keep changing them because another idea would come to me.\n\nI was also looking up genealogy records, just going and going with it, like I had to keep following every little lead right then or I’d lose it. Like there wasn’t an off switch.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others might not share—like thinking you had a special connection to someone famous, or that events in the world were directly related to you in a way that others didn’t understand?\n\nPatient: Not really like having a special connection to someone famous, no. It felt like confidence, like I was finally seeing what needed to happen. But looking back, I can see I was maybe putting way too much certainty on it. I really believed I had figured out the answer for us, and I was frustrated when other people didn’t understand why it seemed so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things in the environment—like things on TV, things people say around you, or even random events—were directed at you in some way, like they had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like the TV was talking to me or a random comment had some hidden meaning for me personally. I mean, sometimes if you’re already worked up about something, you notice things more—like if I’m thinking about crafts, then I’ll see craft ideas everywhere, or if someone mentions money or retirement I might think, “See, that’s exactly what I’ve been saying.” But I don’t think I felt like it was directed at me in some special way.\n\nIt was more that I was focused on my own ideas, if that makes sense. I kept thinking, if everyone would just listen long enough, they’d understand that I had the perfect plan. Like I had solved something for us.\n\nIf someone questioned it or acted unsure, I didn’t really take that in. And I’ll go look, or I’ll ask my husband if he said something, and nobody did.\n\nIt happened once at the warehouse too, and that really shook me because I’m used to noise there, you know—forklifts, people moving around, all that—but this was different. It sounded like my name, clear and close, and when I checked there wasn’t anyone there who could’ve said it. It didn’t sound like a thought in my head. It sounded real, like my husband standing right next to me saying my name. That’s what scared me.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt you had special talents, abilities, or importance that others didn’t recognize, like you were meant for something bigger or had a unique role to play?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I did feel that way, mostly around the crafts idea. I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I don’t think. I haven’t had the feeling that someone was trying to hurt me or plotting against me or anything like that.

But I guess there was a time I got really, really sure about this idea I had, and looking back I can see other people didn’t quite understand why I was so convinced.\n\nI do crafts, you know, little things I make, and I got it in my head that I could turn it into a real business right away. Not just selling a few things here and there, but like… this was going to be our retirement plan, and I had figured it out. But looking back, it was like I couldn’t sit still.\n\nI’d come home from the warehouse after being on my feet all day, and instead of being tired like I normally would be, I’d start tearing through closets or reorganizing things that didn’t really need to be reorganized right then. I’d be pulling boxes out, making piles, deciding we needed a whole new system for things. And then I’d sit down and start rewriting schedules, like household schedules or plans for what needed to get done, and I’d keep changing them because another idea would come to me.\n\nI was also looking up genealogy records, just going and going with it, like I had to keep following every little lead right then or I’d lose it. And I was making lists constantly—lists for the house, lists for projects, lists for things to look up, things to buy, things to fix. It felt like my brain was moving faster than my body, honestly. I’d finish one thing and immediately think of three more things I needed to do.\n\nOther people probably just saw me as being busy or worked up, but inside it felt more intense than that. Like there wasn’t an off switch.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others might not share—like thinking you had a special connection to someone famous, or that events in the world were directly related to you in a way that others didn’t understand?\n\nPatient: Not really like having a special connection to someone famous, no. I didn’t think a celebrity was sending me messages or anything like that. It was more… with my crafts, I got this really strong belief that I had figured out something big.\n\nI make things, and I started thinking I could turn it into a real business almost right away—not just extra money, but something serious, like it could be our retirement plan. And I remember feeling like I had this perfect plan for the family, like all the pieces made sense in my head, and if I could just explain it the right way, everyone else would see it too.\n\nIt’s hard to describe because at the time it didn’t feel strange. It felt like confidence, like I was finally seeing what needed to happen. But looking back, I can see I was maybe putting way too much certainty on it. I really believed I had figured out the answer for us, and I was frustrated when other people didn’t understand why it seemed so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things in the environment—like things on TV, things people say around you, or even random events—were directed at you in some way, like they had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like the TV was talking to me or a random comment had some hidden meaning for me personally. I mean, sometimes if you’re already worked up about something, you notice things more—like if I’m thinking about crafts, then I’ll see craft ideas everywhere, or if someone mentions money or retirement I might think, “See, that’s exactly what I’ve been saying.” But I don’t think I felt like it was directed at me in some special way.\n\nIt was more that I was focused on my own ideas, if that makes sense. Like I was interpreting things through whatever I was already thinking about, but I wasn’t sitting there believing the news or a show or strangers were sending me messages. I don’t remember anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you believed something strongly that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to tell you otherwise, you couldn’t let go of that belief—like thinking something was going to happen, that someone had a particular intention, or that you had a special role to play?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably the crafts thing again, because at the time I really, really believed I had figured something out. It wasn’t just, “Oh, maybe I could sell a few things,” it was more like I had this whole picture in my head where it was going to become a real business right away, like a serious thing, and it could be our retirement plan.\n\nAnd when I say it out loud now, I know that sounds like a big jump. But back then it felt so clear to me. I had ideas for what I would make, how I’d sell it, how the money would build up, how it would help the family. I kept thinking, if everyone would just listen long enough, they’d understand that I had the perfect plan. Like I had solved something for us.\n\nIf someone questioned it or acted unsure, I didn’t really take that in. I’d just think they weren’t seeing the whole picture yet, or I hadn’t explained it the right way. I couldn’t let it go because it felt obvious to me, like I was the only one looking at it correctly. Looking back, I can see I was way too certain about it, but at the time I believed it completely.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other types of unusual perceptions, like smelling something that wasn’t there, tasting something odd without a cause, or feeling physical sensations that had no clear explanation?\n\nPatient: Not so much smells or tastes, no. I can’t think of anything like tasting something strange or smelling smoke or perfume when there wasn’t anything there.\n\nBut… the thing that really scared me, honestly, and part of why I came in, is I’ve been hearing my name called. Like from the hallway at home. I’ll be in another room and I’ll hear it clear as anything, like somebody is standing there calling me. And I’ll go look, or I’ll ask my husband if he said something, and nobody did.\n\nIt happened once at the warehouse too, and that really shook me because I’m used to noise there, you know—forklifts, people moving around, all that—but this was different. It sounded like my name, clear and close, and when I checked there wasn’t anyone there who could’ve said it. It didn’t sound like a thought in my head. It sounded real, like my husband standing right next to me saying my name. That’s what scared me.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt you had special talents, abilities, or importance that others didn’t recognize, like you were meant for something bigger or had a unique role to play?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I did feel that way, mostly around the crafts idea. It felt like I had finally figured out something important, like I saw a way forward that everyone else just wasn’t seeing yet.\n\nI really believed I could turn my crafts into a real business right away, not just a little side hobby. I remember thinking that once people saw what I was making and how I planned it out, they’d understand I had this perfect plan.\n\nAnd if my family didn’t get it right away, I’d feel frustrated, like, “Why can’t they see this?” I wasn’t thinking, “Maybe I’m getting ahead of myself.” I was thinking they just didn’t recognize yet how good the idea was, or how capable I was of making it happen. Looking back, it was a lot more confidence than I usually have, and it got big in my head really fast.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people were watching you, talking about you, or trying to harm you in some way, even if you couldn’t prove it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I don’t think. Looking back, it was a lot more confidence than I usually have, and it got big in my head really fast.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people were watching you, talking about you, or trying to harm you in some way, even if you couldn’t prove it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I don’t think. It’s not like I was convinced of it, and I didn’t have proof or keep following that thought.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say I’ve really felt watched or targeted. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think the crafts thing is probably the clearest example. At the time I really believed I had figured something out that everybody else just couldn’t see yet. Like, in my mind, it wasn’t just, “Oh, maybe I could sell a few things,” it was more like, “No, this is the plan. This is how we’re going to get ahead. This could be a retirement business.”

And I got very convinced of it very fast. I kept thinking, once people saw what I was making, and once they understood the way I had it all pictured in my head, they would realize I had the perfect plan for our family. If someone questioned it or seemed unsure, I didn’t really take that in the way I normally might. I’d feel like they just didn’t understand yet, or they weren’t seeing the bigger picture that I was seeing.

Looking back, it feels kind of embarrassing to say, because I really thought I was being practical and inspired, but it was more than that. I felt like I had this ability or this idea that was going to change things for us right away, and I was so sure everyone would eventually see that I was right.

But I guess there was a time I got really, really sure about this idea I had, and looking back I can see other people didn’t quite understand why I was so convinced.\n\nI do crafts, you know, little things I make, and I got it in my head that I could turn it into a real business right away. Not just selling a few things here and there, but like… this was going to be our retirement plan, and I had figured it out. It didn’t feel strange then. It felt smart, like I was finally seeing the whole picture. But I know my family didn’t react like I expected. I think they thought I was getting ahead of myself.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you strongly believed something that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to explain otherwise, you still felt certain it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think the closest thing would be the crafts idea I was talking about. At the time it didn’t feel like a “belief” the way you’re saying it now—it felt like I had finally figured something out that everyone else just wasn’t catching up to yet.\n\nI make crafts, and I got really convinced that I could turn it into a real business right away. Not slowly, not like a little side thing, but like something that could actually become our retirement plan. I remember thinking, very clearly, that this was the perfect plan for our family, like I had worked out all the pieces in my head and once I explained it properly, everyone would see it too.\n\nAnd when they didn’t… I didn’t take that as a sign that maybe I was overestimating it. I just felt like they weren’t understanding what I was seeing. I was so sure I had figured it out, like it was obvious and they were being too cautious or negative. Looking back, I can see I was probably way more certain than made sense, but at the time I really believed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing, even if others around you didn’t seem to notice or understand what you were experiencing?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that definitely happened. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve called it “unusually energetic” at the time, because I thought I was just finally getting things done, you know? But looking back, it was like I couldn’t sit still.\n\nI’d come home from the warehouse after being on my feet all day, and instead of being tired like I normally would be, I’d start tearing through closets or reorganizing things that didn’t really need to be reorganized right then. I’d be pulling boxes out, making piles, deciding we needed a whole new system for things. And then I’d sit down and start rewriting schedules, like household schedules or plans for what needed to get done, and I’d keep changing them because another idea would come to me.\n\nI was also looking up genealogy records, just going and going with it, like I had to keep following every little lead right then or I’d lose it. And I was making lists constantly—lists for the house, lists for projects, lists for things to look up, things to buy, things to fix. It felt like my brain was moving faster than my body, honestly. I’d finish one thing and immediately think of three more things I needed to do.\n\nOther people probably just saw me as being busy or worked up, but inside it felt more intense than that. Like there wasn’t an off switch.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others might not share—like thinking you had a special connection to someone famous, or that events in the world were directly related to you in a way that others didn’t understand?\n\nPatient: Not really like having a special connection to someone famous, no. I didn’t think a celebrity was sending me messages or anything like that. It was more… with my crafts, I got this really strong belief that I had figured out something big.\n\nI make things, and I started thinking I could turn it into a real business almost right away—not just extra money, but something serious, like it could be our retirement plan. And I remember feeling like I had this perfect plan for the family, like all the pieces made sense in my head, and if I could just explain it the right way, everyone else would see it too.\n\nIt’s hard to describe because at the time it didn’t feel strange. It felt like confidence, like I was finally seeing what needed to happen. But looking back, I can see I was maybe putting way too much certainty on it. I really believed I had figured out the answer for us, and I was frustrated when other people didn’t understand why it seemed so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things in the environment—like things on TV, things people say around you, or even random events—were directed at you in some way, like they had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like the TV was talking to me or a random comment had some hidden meaning for me personally. I mean, sometimes if you’re already worked up about something, you notice things more—like if I’m thinking about crafts, then I’ll see craft ideas everywhere, or if someone mentions money or retirement I might think, “See, that’s exactly what I’ve been saying.” But I don’t think I felt like it was directed at me in some special way.\n\nIt was more that I was focused on my own ideas, if that makes sense. Like I was interpreting things through whatever I was already thinking about, but I wasn’t sitting there believing the news or a show or strangers were sending me messages. I don’t remember anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you believed something strongly that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to tell you otherwise, you couldn’t let go of that belief—like thinking something was going to happen, that someone had a particular intention, or that you had a special role to play?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably the crafts thing again, because at the time I really, really believed I had figured something out. At the time it didn’t feel like a “belief” the way you’re saying it now—it felt like I had finally figured something out that everyone else just wasn’t catching up to yet.\n\nI make crafts, and I got really convinced that I could turn it into a real business right away. Not slowly, not like a little side thing, but like something that could actually become our retirement plan. I remember thinking, very clearly, that this was the perfect plan for our family, like I had worked out all the pieces in my head and once I explained it properly, everyone would see it too.\n\nAnd when they didn’t… I didn’t take that as a sign that maybe I was overestimating it. I just felt like they weren’t understanding what I was seeing. I was so sure I had figured it out, like it was obvious and they were being too cautious or negative. Looking back, I can see I was probably way more certain than made sense, but at the time I really believed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing, even if others around you didn’t seem to notice or understand what you were experiencing?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that definitely happened. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve called it “unusually energetic” at the time, because I thought I was just finally getting things done, you know? But looking back, it was like I couldn’t sit still.\n\nI’d come home from the warehouse after being on my feet all day, and instead of being tired like I normally would be, I’d start tearing through closets or reorganizing things that didn’t really need to be reorganized right then. I’d be pulling boxes out, making piles, deciding we needed a whole new system for things. And then I’d sit down and start rewriting schedules, like household schedules or plans for what needed to get done, and I’d keep changing them because another idea would come to me.\n\nI was also looking up genealogy records, just going and going with it, like I had to keep following every little lead right then or I’d lose it. And I was making lists constantly—lists for the house, lists for projects, lists for things to look up, things to buy, things to fix. It felt like my brain was moving faster than my body, honestly. I’d finish one thing and immediately think of three more things I needed to do.\n\nOther people probably just saw me as being busy or worked up, but inside it felt more intense than that. Like there wasn’t an off switch.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others might not share—like thinking you had a special connection to someone famous, or that events in the world were directly related to you in a way that others didn’t understand?\n\nPatient: Not really like having a special connection to someone famous, no. I didn’t think a celebrity was sending me messages or anything like that. It was more… with my crafts, I got this really strong belief that I had figured out something big.\n\nI make things, and I started thinking I could turn it into a real business almost right away—not just extra money, but something serious, like it could be our retirement plan. And I remember feeling like I had this perfect plan for the family, like all the pieces made sense in my head, and if I could just explain it the right way, everyone else would see it too.\n\nIt’s hard to describe because at the time it didn’t feel strange. It felt like confidence, like I was finally seeing what needed to happen. But looking back, I can see I was maybe putting way too much certainty on it. I really believed I had figured out the answer for us, and I was frustrated when other people didn’t understand why it seemed so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things in the environment—like things on TV, things people say around you, or even random events—were directed at you in some way, like they had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes if you’re already worked up about something, you notice things more—like if I’m thinking about crafts, then I’ll see craft ideas everywhere, or if someone mentions money or retirement I might think, “See, that’s exactly what I’ve been saying.” But I don’t think I felt like it was directed at me in some special way.\n\nIt was more that I was focused on my own ideas, if that makes sense. Like I was interpreting things through whatever I was already thinking about, but I wasn’t sitting there believing the news or a show or strangers were sending me messages. I don’t remember anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you believed something strongly that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to tell you otherwise, you couldn’t let go of that belief—like thinking something was going to happen, that someone had a particular intention, or that you had a special role to play?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably the crafts thing again, because at the time I really, really believed I had figured something out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think so, but for me it was more about what could realistically happen, at least in my mind. It wasn’t like some impossible thing. It was the crafts business.

I got it in my head that I could turn my crafts into a real retirement business right away, like not years down the road or after slowly building it up, but almost like I had figured out this perfect plan for our family. I really believed that once I got it going, everyone would see it too — that I had found a way for us to get ahead, maybe even retire on it someday.

And the thing is, there wasn’t really proof for that. I mean, I had my ideas, and I had things I was making, and in my mind that felt like enough. But if somebody didn’t agree or tried to slow me down, I didn’t really take that as a sign that maybe I was getting carried away. I just felt like they weren’t seeing what I was seeing yet.

So yes, it was something that could happen in real life, I guess — people do turn hobbies into businesses — but I had it built up so big and so fast in my head that I was just completely sure I had figured out the answer.

A couple times I thought it was my husband, because it sounded that close, like he was right next to me or just around the corner, but when I checked, nobody was there.\n\nAnd then it happened once at the warehouse too, where I work, and that really shook me up because I was sure someone had called me, but there wasn’t anyone there. I mean, it sounded as real as if my husband was standing right beside me. That’s what scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other experiences that aren’t normal for you—like seeing things that aren’t there, feeling sensations on your skin, or maybe thoughts that don’t feel like your own?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve seen anything, no… not like seeing a person standing there or something crawling on me. It’s really been the hearing part that’s bothering me.\n\nIt’s my name. I’ll be at home and I’ll hear it from the hallway, clear as anything, like someone is right there just out of sight. And I’ll stop what I’m doing because it doesn’t sound like a thought—it sounds like an actual voice in the house. A couple times I honestly thought it was my husband calling me, because it was that real, like he was standing right next to me or just around the corner. But then I go look, and there’s nobody there.\n\nAnd it happened once at the warehouse too, which really scared me because at work there’s always noises and people moving around, so at first I thought, okay, someone must’ve called me. But when I checked, no one had. That’s the part I can’t shake—how real it sounds when there’s nobody there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that others don’t see, or experienced any other strange sensations like feeling something that wasn’t there?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t seen anything like that, not really. I’m not seeing people or shadows or anything moving that other people don’t see, and I haven’t felt things on my skin or anything like that.\n\nIt’s the hearing that’s been scaring me. I’ll be at home and hear my name called from the hallway, clear enough that I’ll actually stop what I’m doing and answer or go look. It sounds like someone is really there—like my husband is standing right next to me or just around the corner—but then there’s nobody. And it happened once at the warehouse too, which really shook me because I thought someone had called me at work, but nobody had. That’s the part that feels so real and makes me nervous.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts or beliefs that others might find unusual or hard to understand, like thinking someone is trying to harm you or that you have a special ability others don’t recognize?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I thought someone was trying to hurt me, no… not like that. But I guess there was a time I got really, really sure about this idea I had, and looking back I can see other people didn’t quite understand why I was so convinced.\n\nI do crafts, you know, little things I make, and I got it in my head that I could turn it into a real business right away. Not just selling a few things here and there, but like… this was going to be our retirement plan, and I had figured it out. I remember feeling like everyone would see it once I explained it properly, like I had finally found the perfect way to set our family up. It felt obvious to me at the time, like, “Why hasn’t anyone else thought of this?” And I was pretty sure I could make it work fast, not someday way down the line.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as unusual. It didn’t feel strange then. It felt smart, like I was finally seeing the whole picture. But I know my family didn’t react like I expected. I think they thought I was getting ahead of myself.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you strongly believed something that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to explain otherwise, you still felt certain it was?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think the closest thing would be the crafts idea I was talking about. At the time it didn’t feel like a “belief” the way you’re saying it now—it felt like I had finally figured something out that everyone else just wasn’t catching up to yet.\n\nI make crafts, and I got really convinced that I could turn it into a real business right away. Not slowly, not like a little side thing, but like something that could actually become our retirement plan. I remember thinking, very clearly, that this was the perfect plan for our family, like I had worked out all the pieces in my head and once I explained it properly, everyone would see it too.\n\nAnd when they didn’t… I didn’t take that as a sign that maybe I was overestimating it. I just felt like they weren’t understanding what I was seeing. I was so sure I had figured it out, like it was obvious and they were being too cautious or negative. Looking back, I can see I was probably way more certain than made sense, but at the time I really believed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you felt unusually energetic, needed less sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing, even if others around you didn’t seem to notice or understand what you were experiencing?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that definitely happened. I mean, I don’t know if I would’ve called it “unusually energetic” at the time, because I thought I was just finally getting things done, you know? But looking back, it was like I couldn’t sit still.\n\nI’d come home from the warehouse after being on my feet all day, and instead of being tired like I normally would be, I’d start tearing through closets or reorganizing things that didn’t really need to be reorganized right then. I’d be pulling boxes out, making piles, deciding we needed a whole new system for things. And then I’d sit down and start rewriting schedules, like household schedules or plans for what needed to get done, and I’d keep changing them because another idea would come to me.\n\nI was also looking up genealogy records, just going and going with it, like I had to keep following every little lead right then or I’d lose it. And I was making lists constantly—lists for the house, lists for projects, lists for things to look up, things to buy, things to fix. It felt like my brain was moving faster than my body, honestly. I’d finish one thing and immediately think of three more things I needed to do.\n\nOther people probably just saw me as being busy or worked up, but inside it felt more intense than that. Like there wasn’t an off switch.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs that others might not share—like thinking you had a special connection to someone famous, or that events in the world were directly related to you in a way that others didn’t understand?\n\nPatient: Not really like having a special connection to someone famous, no. I didn’t think a celebrity was sending me messages or anything like that. It was more… with my crafts, I got this really strong belief that I had figured out something big.\n\nI make things, and I started thinking I could turn it into a real business almost right away—not just extra money, but something serious, like it could be our retirement plan. And I remember feeling like I had this perfect plan for the family, like all the pieces made sense in my head, and if I could just explain it the right way, everyone else would see it too.\n\nIt’s hard to describe because at the time it didn’t feel strange. It felt like confidence, like I was finally seeing what needed to happen. But looking back, I can see I was maybe putting way too much certainty on it. I really believed I had figured out the answer for us, and I was frustrated when other people didn’t understand why it seemed so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things in the environment—like things on TV, things people say around you, or even random events—were directed at you in some way, like they had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like the TV was talking to me or a random comment had some hidden meaning for me personally. I mean, sometimes if you’re already worked up about something, you notice things more—like if I’m thinking about crafts, then I’ll see craft ideas everywhere, or if someone mentions money or retirement I might think, “See, that’s exactly what I’ve been saying.” But I don’t think I felt like it was directed at me in some special way.\n\nIt was more that I was focused on my own ideas, if that makes sense. Like I was interpreting things through whatever I was already thinking about, but I wasn’t sitting there believing the news or a show or strangers were sending me messages. I don’t remember anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you believed something strongly that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to tell you otherwise, you couldn’t let go of that belief—like thinking something was going to happen, that someone had a particular intention, or that you had a special role to play?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably the crafts thing again, because at the time I really, really believed I had figured something out. It wasn’t just, “Oh, maybe I could sell a few things,” it was more like I had this whole picture in my head where it was going to become a real business right away, like a serious thing, and it could be our retirement plan.\n\nAnd when I say it out loud now, I know that sounds like a big jump. But back then it felt so clear to me. I had ideas for what I would make, how I’d sell it, how the money would build up, how it would help the family. I kept thinking, if everyone would just listen long enough, they’d understand that I had the perfect plan. Like I had solved something for us.\n\nIf someone questioned it or acted unsure, I didn’t really take that in. I’d just think they weren’t seeing the whole picture yet, or I hadn’t explained it the right way. I couldn’t let it go because it felt obvious to me, like I was the only one looking at it correctly. Looking back, I can see I was way too certain about it, but at the time I believed it completely.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other types of unusual perceptions, like smelling something that wasn’t there, tasting something odd without a cause, or feeling physical sensations that had no clear explanation?\n\nPatient: Not so much smells or tastes, no. I can’t think of anything like tasting something strange or smelling smoke or perfume when there wasn’t anything there.\n\nBut… the thing that really scared me, honestly, and part of why I came in, is I’ve been hearing my name called. Like from the hallway at home. I’ll be in another room and I’ll hear it clear as anything, like somebody is standing there calling me. And I’ll go look, or I’ll ask my husband if he said something, and nobody did.\n\nIt happened once at the warehouse too, and that really shook me because I’m used to noise there, you know—forklifts, people moving around, all that—but this was different. It sounded like my name, clear and close, and when I checked there wasn’t anyone there who could’ve said it. It didn’t sound like a thought in my head. It sounded real, like my husband standing right next to me saying my name. That’s what scared me.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt you had special talents, abilities, or importance that others didn’t recognize, like you were meant for something bigger or had a unique role to play?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I did feel that way, mostly around the crafts idea. At the time it didn’t feel like I was being unrealistic or bragging or anything. It felt like I had finally figured out something important, like I saw a way forward that everyone else just wasn’t seeing yet.\n\nI really believed I could turn my crafts into a real business right away, not just a little side hobby. In my head it was going to become this serious thing that could help us retire, help the family, kind of prove that I had found the answer. I remember thinking that once people saw what I was making and how I planned it out, they’d understand I had this perfect plan.\n\nAnd if my family didn’t get it right away, I’d feel frustrated, like, “Why can’t they see this?” I wasn’t thinking, “Maybe I’m getting ahead of myself.” I was thinking they just didn’t recognize yet how good the idea was, or how capable I was of making it happen. Looking back, it was a lot more confidence than I usually have, and it got big in my head really fast.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people were watching you, talking about you, or trying to harm you in some way, even if you couldn’t prove it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I don’t think. I haven’t had the feeling that someone was trying to hurt me or plotting against me or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I can get self-conscious sometimes, like at work if people are talking and then they get quiet, I might wonder for a second if it was about me, but I think that’s more just regular insecurity or embarrassment. It’s not like I was convinced of it, and I didn’t have proof or keep following that thought.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say I’ve really felt watched or targeted. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you believed something strongly that others didn’t share or understand, even when they tried to tell you it wasn’t true—like you had a special role, importance, or abilities that others couldn’t see?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think the crafts thing is probably the clearest example. At the time I really believed I had figured something out that everybody else just couldn’t see yet. Like, in my mind, it wasn’t just, “Oh, maybe I could sell a few things,” it was more like, “No, this is the plan. This is how we’re going to get ahead. This could be a retirement business.”\n\nAnd I got very convinced of it very fast. I kept thinking, once people saw what I was making, and once they understood the way I had it all pictured in my head, they would realize I had the perfect plan for our family. If someone questioned it or seemed unsure, I didn’t really take that in the way I normally might. I’d feel like they just didn’t understand yet, or they weren’t seeing the bigger picture that I was seeing.\n\nLooking back, it feels kind of embarrassing to say, because I really thought I was being practical and inspired, but it was more than that. I felt like I had this ability or this idea that was going to change things for us right away, and I was so sure everyone would eventually see that I was right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had strong beliefs that lasted for a long time, even when others didn’t agree or when there wasn’t clear proof, but those beliefs were about things that could happen in real life—like having a special talent, being followed, or someone being in love with you—rather than things that are completely impossible or strange?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, but for me it was more about what could realistically happen, at least in my mind. It wasn’t like some impossible thing. It was the crafts business.\n\nI got it in my head that I could turn my crafts into a real retirement business right away, like not years down the road or after slowly building it up, but almost like I had figured out this perfect plan for our family. I really believed that once I got it going, everyone would see it too — that I had found a way for us to get ahead, maybe even retire on it someday.\n\nAnd the thing is, there wasn’t really proof for that. I mean, I had my ideas, and I had things I was making, and in my mind that felt like enough. But if somebody didn’t agree or tried to slow me down, I didn’t really take that as a sign that maybe I was getting carried away. I just felt like they weren’t seeing what I was seeing yet.\n\nSo yes, it was something that could happen in real life, I guess — people do turn hobbies into businesses — but I had it built up so big and so fast in my head that I was just completely sure I had figured out the answer.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… that’s actually one of the things that scared me enough to come in.

I’ve been hearing my name called when there’s nobody there. Mostly at home, like from the hallway. I’ll be in another room and I’ll hear it clear as anything, like someone is standing right there and calling me. It doesn’t sound fuzzy or like it could be the TV or a pipe or something settling in the house. It sounds real — like my husband is right next to me saying my name.

And there was one time at the warehouse, too, when I heard my name called and I looked around, but nobody was there. That one really shook me up because at home I can at least try to explain it away for a second, like maybe someone was in the hall or maybe I misheard something. But at work, when I checked and there wasn’t anybody around, it made me feel… I don’t know, frightened and embarrassed, I guess.

It’s not constant, but when it happens it feels very real in the moment. I don’t want to say that lightly, because I know how it sounds, but yes, I’ve heard my name when there wasn’t anyone there calling me.

I’d finish one thing and immediately think of three more things I needed to do.\n\nOther people probably just saw me as being busy or worked up, but inside it felt more intense than that. I didn’t think a celebrity was sending me messages or anything like that.
