And I remember feeling like I had this perfect plan for the family, like all the pieces made sense in my head, and if I could just explain it the right way, everyone else would see it too.\n\nIt’s hard to describe because at the time it didn’t feel strange. It felt like confidence, like I was finally seeing what needed to happen. But looking back, I can see I was maybe putting way too much certainty on it. I really believed I had figured out the answer for us, and I was frustrated when other people didn’t understand why it seemed so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things in the environment—like things on TV, things people say around you, or even random events—were directed at you in some way, like they had a special meaning just for you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like the TV was talking to me or a random comment had some hidden meaning for me personally. I mean, sometimes if you’re already worked up about something, you notice things more—like if I’m thinking about crafts, then I’ll see craft ideas everywhere, or if someone mentions money or retirement I might think, “See, that’s exactly what I’ve been saying.” But I don’t think I felt like it was directed at me in some special way.\n\nIt was more that I was focused on my own ideas, if that makes sense. Like I was interpreting things through whatever I was already thinking about, but I wasn’t sitting there believing the news or a show or strangers were sending me messages. I don’t remember anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you believed something strongly that turned out not to be true, and even when people tried to tell you otherwise, you couldn’t let go of that belief—like thinking something was going to happen, that someone had a particular intention, or that you had a special role to play?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably the crafts thing again, because at the time I really, really believed I had figured something out. It wasn’t just, “Oh, maybe I could sell a few things,” it was more like I had this whole picture in my head where it was going to become a real business right away, like a serious thing, and it could be our retirement plan.\n\nAnd when I say it out loud now, I know that sounds like a big jump. But back then it felt so clear to me. I had ideas for what I would make, how I’d sell it, how the money would build up, how it would help the family. I kept thinking, if everyone would just listen long enough, they’d understand that I had the perfect plan. Like I had solved something for us.\n\nIf someone questioned it or acted unsure, I didn’t really take that in. I’d just think they weren’t seeing the whole picture yet, or I hadn’t explained it the right way. I couldn’t let it go because it felt obvious to me, like I was the only one looking at it correctly. Looking back, I can see I was way too certain about it, but at the time I believed it completely.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any other types of unusual perceptions, like smelling something that wasn’t there, tasting something odd without a cause, or feeling physical sensations that had no clear explanation?\n\nPatient: Not so much smells or tastes, no. I can’t think of anything like tasting something strange or smelling smoke or perfume when there wasn’t anything there.\n\nBut… the thing that really scared me, honestly, and part of why I came in, is I’ve been hearing my name called. Like from the hallway at home. I’ll be in another room and I’ll hear it clear as anything, like somebody is standing there calling me. And I’ll go look, or I’ll ask my husband if he said something, and nobody did.\n\nIt happened once at the warehouse too, and that really shook me because I’m used to noise there, you know—forklifts, people moving around, all that—but this was different. It sounded like my name, clear and close, and when I checked there wasn’t anyone there who could’ve said it. It didn’t sound like a thought in my head. It sounded real, like my husband standing right next to me saying my name. That’s what scared me.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt you had special talents, abilities, or importance that others didn’t recognize, like you were meant for something bigger or had a unique role to play?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I did feel that way, mostly around the crafts idea. At the time it didn’t feel like I was being unrealistic or bragging or anything. It felt like I had finally figured out something important, like I saw a way forward that everyone else just wasn’t seeing yet.\n\nI really believed I could turn my crafts into a real business right away, not just a little side hobby. In my head it was going to become this serious thing that could help us retire, help the family, kind of prove that I had found the answer. I remember thinking that once people saw what I was making and how I planned it out, they’d understand I had this perfect plan.\n\nAnd if my family didn’t get it right away, I’d feel frustrated, like, “Why can’t they see this?” I wasn’t thinking, “Maybe I’m getting ahead of myself.” I was thinking they just didn’t recognize yet how good the idea was, or how capable I was of making it happen. Looking back, it was a lot more confidence than I usually have, and it got big in my head really fast.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people were watching you, talking about you, or trying to harm you in some way, even if you couldn’t prove it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, I don’t think. I haven’t had the feeling that someone was trying to hurt me or plotting against me or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I can get self-conscious sometimes, like at work if people are talking and then they get quiet, I might wonder for a second if it was about me, but I think that’s more just regular insecurity or embarrassment. It’s not like I was convinced of it, and I didn’t have proof or keep following that thought.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t say I’ve really felt watched or targeted. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you believed something strongly that others didn’t share or understand, even when they tried to tell you it wasn’t true—like you had a special role, importance, or abilities that others couldn’t see?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think the crafts thing is probably the clearest example. At the time I really believed I had figured something out that everybody else just couldn’t see yet. Like, in my mind, it wasn’t just, “Oh, maybe I could sell a few things,” it was more like, “No, this is the plan. This is how we’re going to get ahead. This could be a retirement business.”\n\nAnd I got very convinced of it very fast. If someone questioned it or seemed unsure, I didn’t really take that in the way I normally might. I’d feel like they just didn’t understand yet, or they weren’t seeing the bigger picture that I was seeing.\n\nLooking back, it feels kind of embarrassing to say, because I really thought I was being practical and inspired, but it was more than that. I felt like I had this ability or this idea that was going to change things for us right away, and I was so sure everyone would eventually see that I was right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had strong beliefs that lasted for a long time, even when others didn’t agree or when there wasn’t clear proof, but those beliefs were about things that could happen in real life—like having a special talent, being followed, or someone being in love with you—rather than things that are completely impossible or strange?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, but for me it was more about what could realistically happen, at least in my mind. I just felt like they weren’t seeing what I was seeing yet.\n\nSo yes, it was something that could happen in real life, I guess — people do turn hobbies into businesses — but I had it built up so big and so fast in my head that I was just completely sure I had figured out the answer.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that you knew weren’t coming from anywhere in the room, and weren’t related to something like stress, lack of sleep, or hearing something in the background?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s actually one of the things that scared me enough to come in.\n\nI’ve been hearing my name called when there’s nobody there. Mostly at home, like from the hallway. I’ll be in another room and I’ll hear it clear as anything, like someone is standing right there and calling me.     "additional_requirements": [
      "Auditory hallucinations occur in the context of delusional beliefs, not exclusively during a mood episode",
      "Delusions are non-bizarre and persistent",
      "Presence of a manic episode concurrent with psychotic symptoms"
    ]
  }
}
